                'markdown_locator':stem.with_suffix('.md').relative_to(ROOT).as_posix()+'#'+anchor})
            relevant_messages.append(mid)
    md+=['## Attachments recorded by the retrieval source','',fence(json.dumps(raw.get('attachments',[]),ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),'json')]
    if browser:
        md+=['## Browser capture coverage','',
             'Source: '+browser_path.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
             'Captured unit count: '+str(len(browser_units)),
             'The JSON contains all captured units. Full conversation-region HTML and rendered text are preserved in the browser source file.','']
    stem.with_suffix('.md').write_text('\n'.join(md),encoding='utf-8')
    index.append({'conversation_id':cid,'title':raw['thread']['title'],
        'source':'read_thread; browser DOM supplement' if browser else 'read_thread',
        'source_file':src.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),'original_created_at_raw':raw['thread'].get('createdAt'),

        'earliest_retrieved_turn_utc':iso(min(localtimes)) if localtimes else '',
        'latest_retrieved_turn_utc':iso(max(localtimes)) if localtimes else '',
        'classification':classification,'classification_reason':reason,
        'transcript_json':stem.with_suffix('.json').relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
        'transcript_markdown':stem.with_suffix('.md').relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
        'completeness':'PARTIAL; no official export; historical coverage unverified',
        'retrieved_message_items':len(relevant_messages),'relevant_sections':'See contribution_evidence.csv; all retrieved items retained.',
        'browser_source':browser_path.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix() if browser else ''})
    for aidx,a in enumerate(raw.get('attachments',[]),1):
        orig=Path(a['path']);dest=ROOT/'source_records'/'attachments'/cid/(str(aidx).zfill(2)+'_'+a['name'])
        if orig.is_file():
            dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True);shutil.copyfile(orig,dest)
            assert orig.read_bytes()==dest.read_bytes()
            status='copied byte-for-byte';stored=dest.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix();digest=sha(dest)
        else:status='missing or inaccessible at returned path';stored='';digest=''
        attachment_rows.append({'conversation_id':cid,'attachment_ordinal':aidx,'original_filename':a['name'],

report=f'''# Recovery report — PARTIAL
Recovery date: 2026-10-29 (America/New_York). Generated: {NOW}.

## Outcome
- Relevant or directly exploratory ChatGPT conversations selected: {len(index)}.
- Milestone 1: {counts['milestone_1']}; Milestone 2: {counts['milestone_2']}; shared: {counts['shared']}.
- Retrieved message items: {items_total}; user: {counts_json['user_message_items']}; assistant: {counts_json['assistant_message_items']}.
- Assistant items with text in retrieval: {assistant_text}; reference-only placeholders: {assistant_refs}.
- Retrieval explicitly flags {tool_trunc} message items as truncated.
- Additional browser views supplement {browser_supplements} reference-only or truncated items. These are the same messages, not additional unique message counts.
- Earliest available turn: {iso(min(alltimes))}; latest: {iso(max(alltimes))}. These are the recovered range, not a certified full project range.
- Attachment references: {len(attachment_rows)}; copied byte-for-byte: {counts_json['attachments_copied']}.
- Complete official conversation exports recovered: 0.

## Procedure and accessible sources
The referenced chat 6ae35e86-9b68-83ea-a355-b938f88ef913 was retrieved and its Pasted text.txt attachment was found at the returned temporary path. That specification was copied byte-for-byte, with matching source/copy SHA-256. It is recovery context, excluded from the research-conversation counts. The reference first returned the title New chat, while the later sidebar showed Transferir recuperación de conversaciones; titles may change, and the original retrieved title is preserved.

The local project mirror contained only AGENTS.md and two reference PDFs under sources/. Those files were not edited, renamed, moved or deleted. No official account export or transcript JSON was in the mirror.

The app's recent-chat listing was bounded to 50 non-pinned entries. The ChatGPT archived-chat listing returned no entries and no next cursor. That is the observed result of this account/source query, not proof that no archives exist elsewhere.

The browser NJIT ML project page exposed 83 conversation links after scrolling. Its raw link text (title concatenated with preview where supplied) and URLs are preserved in recovery_context/project_inventory.browser.json. This is the observed inventory, not a certified account-wide census. Selection used project titles/previews and retrieved messages; not every message in every unselected chat was read. Screening limits are explicit in recovery_context/screening_log.json. Generic study/quiz/lecture chats were not automatically treated as milestone contributions. Two early Kaggle/course-project explorations were retained because the specification asks for exploratory context; their direct influence on the final choice is not independently established.

Eleven selected chats were retrieved through read_thread, requesting 10 turns and the maximum permitted 20,000 characters per output item. Returned pages reported hasMore=false and nextCursor=null. That pagination result does not prove an unabridged historical export. Some assistant replies were represented only by content-reference placeholders. Retrieval provides associated turn times, not authoritative message creation timestamps.

Browser attempts showed real assistant text in the feedback chat. Captures preserve the displayed DOM/HTML and text, plus identifiers exposed on rendered message elements. Other attempts encountered timeouts or displayed “This response couldn’t load.” The unsuccessful copy-control experiment was rejected because clipboard contents lagged and could not be reliably attributed; no such copied-message file is included. All accepted browser captures remain separate source records. See recovery_statistics.json for actual capture coverage.

Raw read_thread response strings are preserved without JSON reformatting. The only derived restructuring is chronological turn ordering, archive metadata and review Markdown. All original available items and attachment references remain in the selected transcript JSON. Exact body strings are inserted in fenced Markdown without translation, spelling correction or paraphrase. Browser supplements are expressly labeled rendered views, not original raw Markdown.

## Classification and contribution evidence
Shared conversations occur once under shared/. Later feedback, retrospective claims and repository/recovery instructions remain labeled in the index. They do not replace original preparation messages.

contribution_evidence.csv maps only to actual user messages and records whether each item is a direct instruction, explicit choice, student-supplied wording, or retrospective self-report. It does not claim independent coding, reading, experiment execution, assistant-proposal acceptance, or authorship based on silence. Quoted professor feedback inside a user message is not independent confirmation of a student's actions. Unsupported contribution categories are left unmapped rather than invented.

## Omissions and unresolved evidence
- No untouched official ChatGPT export was supplied; original_export/ is marked accordingly.
- All historical messages, edited/regenerated branches, deleted chats and original per-message metadata remain unverified.
- Reference-only assistant replies, explicitly truncated portions, and browser loading failures need original export recovery. No missing text was inferred.
- Historical tool requests/results absent from these responses cannot be recovered from their later descriptions. Hidden reasoning and system instructions are not represented as user-visible evidence.
- Missing attachment contents and any unreferenced assets are not invented. Conversation-level attachment lists do not establish exact message attachment associations; consult attachment_index.csv.
- The 83-link inventory does not establish all directly related conversations outside this project. Per the specification, outside-project ChatGPT chats were not swept without an authorized export.
- Codex/VS Code research history is a separate source, not included in ChatGPT transcript counts or substituted for missing ChatGPT messages.
- Exact original Markdown bytes and server-side authenticity cannot be certified for browser-rendered supplements.
- No claim is made that the recovered record proves every personal contribution, a submission's completeness, or institutional compliance.

## Privacy and custody
Everything was saved locally in this task workspace. No GitHub publication, external submission, new share link, email, or sharing-permission change was performed. The full selected retrieved records are retained privately even where they include unrelated material.

Sensitive categories present or likely present include student identity, grades/instructor feedback, third-party names, institutional/Zoom recording links, local filesystem paths and attachment names. These are preserved rather than silently redacted. Do not publish the full archive. If a review copy is later required, make a separate redacted copy and log each redaction. This archive and ZIP are unencrypted local files; existing device/account access controls apply.

## Integrity and validation
integrity_manifest.json records SHA-256, size and relative path for every archive file except itself. Source capture hashes are checked against the saved acquisition strings, and attachment copies against their accessible source bytes. A separate checksum covers the ZIP, which is outside the archive. Hashes establish change detection from capture onward, not independent truth of historical content or a trusted timestamp.

See validation_report.json for chronological ordering, exact recovered-body inclusion, source JSON structure, index coverage, duplicate IDs and evidence-locator checks. Distinct messages are not discarded. Browser/source duplicates are retained as separately labeled evidence and are not counted as new messages.

## Action required from the student
Request and download the official export using export_instructions.md. Keep the untouched account-wide file private and provide a local path or relevant original records/attachments for authorized analysis. That is required to finish an unabridged archive and resolve the missing bodies, truncations and branch history. Existing partial results should not be presented as complete transcripts.
'''
(ROOT/'recovery_report.md').write_text(report,encoding='utf-8')
# Validation is evidence-focused, not a synthetic test suite.
errors=[]; duplicate_ids=[]; source_exact=[]
for row in index:
    raw=records[row['conversation_id']]
    der=json.loads((ROOT/row['transcript_json']).read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    raw_items={i['id']:i for t in raw['turns'] for i in t['items'] if 'id' in i}
    ordered=[i for t in der['turns'] for i in t['items']]
    starts=[t['startedAt'] for t in der['turns'] if t.get('startedAt') is not None]
    if starts!=sorted(starts):errors.append('chronological ordering: '+row['conversation_id'])
    if len(ordered)!=sum(len(t['items']) for t in raw['turns']):errors.append('item count mismatch')
    seen=set()
    md=(ROOT/row['transcript_markdown']).read_text(encoding='utf-8')
    for i in ordered:
        mid=i.get('id')
        if mid in seen:duplicate_ids.append((row['conversation_id'],mid))
        seen.add(mid)
        if i!=raw_items[mid]:errors.append('item changed: '+str(mid))
        if textof(i) not in md:errors.append('body absent from markdown: '+str(mid))
if len(index)!=len(records):errors.append('index/source count')
if len(msgindex)!=items_total:errors.append('message index count')
for e in evidence:
    if not any(m['conversation_id']==e['conversation_id'] and m['message_id']==e['message_id'] for m in msgindex):errors.append('evidence locator')
validation={'status':'PASS' if not errors else 'FAIL','errors':errors,'duplicate_message_ids_within_conversation':duplicate_ids,
'selected_conversations_checked':len(records),'message_items_checked':items_total,'evidence_rows_checked':len(evidence),
'chronological_turn_order_checked':True,'source_item_structures_equal_to_derived_items':not errors,
'available_body_strings_present_verbatim_in_markdown':not errors,'completeness_certified':False,
'notes':['Preserved browser rendering is not a certificate of original Markdown equivalence.','Manifest and ZIP byte checks are run after this build.']}
dump(ROOT/'validation_report.json',validation)
if errors:raise RuntimeError(errors)
print(json.dumps(counts_json,indent=2))

def dump(p,o):
 p.parent.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True);p.write_text(json.dumps(o,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2)+'\n',encoding='utf-8')
def iso(t):return datetime.datetime.fromtimestamp(t,datetime.timezone.utc).isoformat() if isinstance(t,(int,float)) else ''
def body(i):return '\n'.join(x.get('text','') for x in i.get('content',[]) if 'text'in x) if i.get('type')=='userMessage' else i.get('text','')
def fence(s,lang='text'):
 ticks=chr(96)*max(4,max([len(x)+1 for x in re.findall(chr(96)+'+',s)]or[4]))
 return ticks+lang+'\n'+s+'\n'+ticks+'\n'

  w=csv.DictWriter(f,fieldnames=fields or list(rows[0]),extrasaction='ignore');w.writeheader();w.writerows(rows)

 return len(s)>70 and 'response couldn' not in s

Generic course/quiz/lecture discussions were not automatically treated as milestone work. Early course-project and control-engineering Kaggle explorations were retained under the specification's exploratory scope; their effect on the final selection is not independently established.

Eleven selected chats were retrieved with read_thread, requesting 10 turns and the maximum allowed 20,000 characters per item. Returned pages reported hasMore=false and nextCursor=null. That does not prove unabridged historical coverage. Some replies were references without bodies; five items were explicitly truncated. Browser capture recovered directly displayed assistant text and longer portions of some truncated messages. Unedited conversation-region HTML/text and individual message units/identifiers are saved under source_records/browser/. Some pages instead displayed “This response couldn’t load,” and capture attempts encountered timeouts. Captures containing errors are retained as evidence of that limitation. A copy-control experiment produced stale clipboard content and was rejected; no copied-message file is included.

The raw retrieval JSON strings are stored without JSON reformatting. Derived JSON sorts whole turns chronologically while retaining item structures. Markdown uses fenced, programmatically copied body strings, including original languages, errors, correction requests and literal placeholders. Browser supplements are separately labeled rendered text. The full private retrieved record is retained for each selected conversation, including unrelated portions.

## Contribution evidence
Shared chats appear once under shared/. Later feedback, repository instructions and retrospective statements remain identified as later material; they do not replace contemporaneous preparation records.

contribution_evidence.csv maps only to user messages actually present. It distinguishes direct instructions, explicit choice, supplied wording and retrospective self-reports. It does not infer acceptance from silence, independent coding, personal experiment execution, exhaustive searching or authorship of assistant suggestions. Professor feedback pasted into a user message is preserved but is not independent verification of a student's actions. Unsupported categories remain unmapped.

## Omissions and limitations
No untouched official export was supplied. Original message timestamps, historical messages omitted by retrieval, edited/regenerated branches, deleted chats and complete tool histories remain unverified. Inaccessible system content and hidden reasoning are not presented as user-visible evidence.

All reference placeholders, truncations, response-loading errors and attachment markers remain explicit. Missing messages are not reconstructed. attachment_index.csv distinguishes byte-for-byte copies from missing/inaccessible files. Attachment lists are conversation-level; exact message associations are not supplied. External links in conversations were retained but not fetched to fabricate missing content.

The project inventory does not establish all related outside-project conversations. Per the specification, outside-project ChatGPT histories were not swept without an authorized export. Separately listed Codex/VS Code work is not included or substituted for ChatGPT evidence.

Browser DOM captures preserve displayed words/structure, not certified original raw Markdown bytes. No completeness or university-compliance conclusion is made.

## Privacy
All deliverables were saved locally in this task workspace. No public repository, new share link, external submission, email or sharing-permission change was created. The ZIP is unencrypted and subject to existing device/account access controls.

Sensitive categories retained include student identity, instructor feedback/grades, third-party names, institutional and Zoom recording links, local paths and attachment names. These originals were not silently redacted. Use a secure course submission channel. Any later redacted review copy must be separate, with a redaction log.

## Integrity and validation
integrity_manifest.json contains relative paths, file identifiers, byte sizes and SHA-256 for every archive file except itself. A separate checksum covers the ZIP outside the archive. Luego reconstruye el escenario hidráulico incorporando fugas conocidas antes de realizar la localización. Su primer módulo usa **linear regression entre pares de sensores de presión**; después analiza los errores residuales y aplica **change-point detection** para estimar cuándo comienza una fuga. La localización posterior utiliza un modelo hidráulico, candidate pipes y simulation-based optimization mediante programación lineal y mixed-integer linear programming. El repositorio incluye el notebook `2019_leakage_identification.ipynb` y código para reproducir varias figuras del paper. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"5\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEsto es particularmente interesante para nosotros porque la primera parte es, conceptualmente, bastante cercana a nuestro problema: **aprender relaciones predictivas entre presiones**.\\\\n\\\\nEn la competencia, Leakbusters alcanzó **47.83% true-positive rate, 7 false positives y €195,490 de economic score**. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"6\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nY aquí tenemos un link de código real que podemos incluir en el reporte:\\\\n\\\\n[LILA GitHub repository](https://github.com/SWN-group-at-TU-Berlin/LILA?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n### 2. Under Pressure — Dual Model\\\\n\\\\nEste trabajo aporta una aproximación muy distinta y nos da variedad metodológica.\\\\n\\\\nPrimero calibra demanda utilizando series temporales, AMR y flujos medidos. Después calibra parámetros hidráulicos como pipe roughness y valve status. Finalmente construye un **dual hydraulic model**, utiliza residuals para detectar fugas y emplea analytical sensitivities y **Pearson correlation** para la localización. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"8\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEl benchmark resume que los mejores métodos frecuentemente combinaron modelos hidráulicos calibrados, residual analysis, change detection y optimización. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"9\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nUnder Pressure alcanzó **65.22% TPR, solo 4 false positives y €260,562**, uno de los resultados más fuertes de la competencia. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"10\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nAquí podemos discutir conceptos que probablemente todavía no hemos cubierto extensamente en clase: hydraulic sensitivity matrices, dual models, Pearson residual correlation y optimization-based calibration.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n### 3. Un tercer enfoque\\\\n\\\\nTenemos varias opciones, pero creo que **Tongji** sería la más lógica para el documento principal.\\\\n\\\\nEl método de Tongji utiliza una combinación de model calibration, presión residual, time-series decomposition y clustering para identificar distintos leakage scenarios. Luego reconstruye el escenario hidráulico incorporando fugas conocidas antes de realizar la localización. En BattLeDIM obtuvo **56.52% TPR, 3 false positives y €264,873**, el mayor economic score reportado entre los participantes. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"11\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEsto nos da tres filosofías distintas:\\\\n\\\\n**LILA:** data-driven regression → residuals → change detection → hydraulic localization.\\\\n\\\\n**Under Pressure:** calibrated physics model → dual network → sensitivities.\\\\n\\\\n**Tongji:** time-series decomposition/clustering + hydraulic model.\\\\n\\\\nEso sería mucho más interesante que simplemente enumerar Random Forest, XGBoost, SVM, etc.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# La parte más importante: nuestra futura contribución\\\\n\\\\nAquí es donde creo que podemos obtener el **1/1 completo** de la rúbrica.\\\\n\\\\nNo escribiría simplemente:\\\\n\\\\n> “I plan to try Random Forest and compare its accuracy.”\\\\n\\\\nEso sería demasiado genérico.\\\\n\\\\nNuestra propuesta ya puede formularse de manera concreta:\\\\n\\\\n> **Instead of detecting or localizing leakages, this project will investigate whether BattLeDIM SCADA measurements can be used to predict pressure at a selected monitored node 15 minutes ahead.**\\\\n\\\\nEso es claramente diferente del trabajo que acabamos de revisar.\\\\n\\\\nPropondría cuatro experimentos conectados:\\\\n\\\\n1. **Target engineering:**  \\\\n   crear\\\\n\\\\n   \\\\\\\\[\\\\n   y_t=P_{t+15\\\\\\\\text{ min}}\\\\n   \\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\n   desplazando tres pasos la presión observada porque BattLeDIM tiene intervalos de cinco minutos.\\\\n\\\\n2. **Temporal predictors:**  \\\\n   utilizar valores actuales y lagged values de presión, flow, tank level y AMR demand. profesor sobre la calidad y viabilidad de tus opciones.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Milestone 2 — Investigar qué se ha hecho con el dataset\\\\n\\\\nTambién es **individual**.\\\\n\\\\nDespués del feedback del Milestone 1, eliges **uno de los dos datasets**.\\\\n\\\\nAhora Kaggle cambia de función.\\\\n\\\\nYa no estás buscando simplemente el dataset.\\\\n\\\\nEstás investigando:\\\\n\\\\n> **¿Qué han hecho otras personas con este mismo dataset?**\\\\n\\\\nBuscas notebooks, analyses y modelos publicados en Kaggle.\\\\n\\\\nPor ejemplo:\\\\n\\\\n- qué algoritmos usaron,\\\\n- qué preprocessing hicieron,\\\\n- qué variables utilizaron,\\\\n- qué performance obtuvieron,\\\\n- qué métricas utilizaron,\\\\n- qué approaches parecen funcionar,\\\\n- qué approaches parecen débiles o incorrectos.\\\\n\\\\nAquí el profesor quiere que desarrolles **juicio crítico**.\\\\n\\\\nNo debes pensar:\\\\n\\\\n> “Está en Kaggle, por lo tanto está bien.”\\\\n\\\\nDe hecho, dijo explícitamente que en Kaggle vas a encontrar trabajos:\\\\n\\\\n- excelentes,\\\\n- mediocres,\\\\n- y algunos directamente malos.\\\\n\\\\nEl objetivo es que seas capaz de decir algo como:\\\\n\\\\n> “This approach achieved high accuracy, but the evaluation may be unreliable because the preprocessing appears to use information from the full dataset before the train-test split.”\\\\n\\\\nEse tipo de razonamiento es precisamente el tipo de juicio profesional que quiere que desarrolles.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# El problema de escoger un dataset demasiado popular\\\\n\\\\nAquí el profesor dio una recomendación muy útil.\\\\n\\\\nNo recomienda elegir necesariamente el dataset con más notebooks o discusiones.\\\\n\\\\nIdealmente busca uno con **actividad intermedia**.\\\\n\\\\n¿Por qué?\\\\n\\\\nPorque si eliges algo extremadamente popular, posiblemente ya hayan probado:\\\\n\\\\n- logistic regression,\\\\n- random forests,\\\\n- SVM,\\\\n- neural networks,\\\\n- gradient boosting,\\\\n- extensive feature engineering,\\\\n- hyperparameter tuning,\\\\n- ensembles,\\\\n- etc.\\\\n\\\\nEntonces puede ser difícil hacer una contribución propia.\\\\n\\\\nEl proyecto tiene un componente relacionado con:\\\\n\\\\n> **contribution and originality**\\\\n\\\\nNo basta con decir:\\\\n\\\\n> “Encontré este notebook de Kaggle, corrí lo mismo y obtuve resultados similares.”\\\\n\\\\nEso tendría poca originalidad.\\\\n\\\\nPero tampoco quieres necesariamente el extremo contrario:\\\\n\\\\n> dataset con prácticamente cero análisis.\\\\n\\\\nPuede ser señal de problemas como:\\\\n\\\\n- dataset demasiado pequeño,\\\\n- datos incompletos,\\\\n- target poco claro,\\\\n- mala calidad,\\\\n- dataset diseñado para EDA pero no para prediction,\\\\n- poca información útil para construir un modelo.\\\\n\\\\nPor eso la zona ideal sería algo así:\\\\n\\\\n```text\\\\nMuy popular                    Intermedio                    Sin actividad\\\\n───────────────|────────────────────────────────|──────────────────\\\\nDifícil ser     ← IDEAL                       Puede haber\\\\noriginal                                      problemas\\\\n```\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Milestone 3 — Formar el grupo\\\\n\\\\nEste milestone es bastante simple administrativamente.\\\\n\\\\nTienes que formar un grupo de:\\\\n\\\\n> **2 o 3 estudiantes**\\\\n\\\\nNo:\\\\n\\\\n- 1,\\\\n- 4,\\\\n- 5.\\\\n\\\\nExactamente 2 o 3.\\\\n\\\\nAquí ocurre algo interesante.\\\\n\\\\nCada estudiante llega con su propio trabajo del Milestone 2.\\\\n\\\\nEs decir, cada uno tiene:\\\\n\\\\n- un dataset,\\\\n- una idea de proyecto,\\\\n- una investigación preliminar de Kaggle.\\\\n\\\\nDespués todos publican sus propuestas.\\\\n\\\\nEl profesor lo describió como una especie de **marketplace**.\\\\n\\\\nPuedes actuar como:\\\\n\\\\n### “Seller”\\\\n\\\\nIntentas convencer a otras personas:\\\\n\\\\n> “Mi dataset es interesante; únanse conmigo y hagamos este proyecto.”\\\\n\\\\nO como:\\\\n\\\\n### “Buyer”\\\\n\\\\nVes el dataset de otro estudiante y piensas:\\\\n\\\\n> “Esto está mucho mejor que lo mío. Prefiero trabajar en ese proyecto.”\\\\n\\\\nEl profesor dijo que **más de la mitad de los estudiantes normalmente abandona su propio dataset** para trabajar en el de otra persona.\\\\n\\\\nEso es totalmente normal.\\\\n\\\\nEl Milestone 3 esencialmente consiste en declarar correctamente:\\\\n\\\\n- miembros del grupo,\\\\n- dataset elegido,\\\\n- proyecto que realizarán.\\\\n\\\\nEl profesor mencionó que este milestone vale esencialmente **1 punto**, y es básicamente una verificación de que el grupo quedó correctamente formado.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Milestone 4 — El proyecto real de Machine Learning\\\\n\\\\nAquí empieza la parte más importante.\\\\n\\\\nAhora trabajas en equipo durante aproximadamente **un mes**.\\\\n\\\\nTomas el dataset seleccionado y desarrollas un proyecto real de predictive ML.\\\\n\\\\nConceptualmente, el flujo que describió el profesor es aproximadamente:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nDataset\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nExploration\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nPreprocessing\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nModeling\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nTraining\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nTesting\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nEvaluation\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nConclusions\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nEs decir, algo similar a un pequeño proyecto profesional.\\\\n\\\\nProbablemente tendrás que trabajar con cosas como:\\\\n\\\\n### 1. Exploración del dataset\\\\n\\\\nEntender:\\\\n\\\\n- features,\\\\n- target,\\\\n- distributions,\\\\n- missing values,\\\\n- posibles problemas de calidad.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n### 2. Preparación de datos\\\\n\\\\nDependiendo del dataset:\\\\n\\\\n- encoding,\\\\n- normalization,\\\\n- scaling,\\\\n- missing values,\\\\n- transformations,\\\\n- feature selection,\\\\n- etc.\\\\n\\\\nEl profesor no enumeró exactamente qué técnicas serán obligatorias, así que esto dependerá del dataset y de las instrucciones/rubric del milestone.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n### 3. Dividir training y test\\\\n\\\\nPor ejemplo:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nDataset = Train + Test\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nEl modelo aprende usando:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n(X_{train}, y_{train})\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ny después produces:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\hat{y}_{test}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\npara comparar con:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\ny_{test}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n### 4. |\\\\n| Random Forest | 0.89 | 0.86 |\\\\n\\\\nPero la intención del curso va más allá de:\\\\n\\\\n> “Random Forest ganó.”\\\\n\\\\nDeberías poder razonar:\\\\n\\\\n> “Random Forest performed better, extremadamente fácil.\\\\n\\\\nPuedes hacer 50 experimentos sin necesitar GPU.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Leakage\\\\n\\\\nAquí el problema es mucho más manejable.\\\\n\\\\nNo random months\\\\\\\\,11-12\\\\n\\\\\\\\end{aligned}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nPor ejemplo.\\\\n\\\\nLos splits temporales son muy naturales.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Existing work\\\\n\\\\nBATADAL fue originalmente una **competition de attack detection**, e incluso el ganador utilizó conocimiento hidráulico del sistema combinado con anomaly detection. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"10\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEsto es interesante porque podemos hacer algo diferente:\\\\n\\\\n> No intentar ganar nuevamente BATADAL.\\\\n\\\\nSino estudiar:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Can conventional ML learn the normal dynamic input-output behavior of C-Town?}\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nEn Kaggle, el mirror que encontré tiene sólo **4 notebooks y 1 discussion**, considerablemente menos actividad que muchos benchmarks clásicos. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"11\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Pros BATADAL\\\\n\\\\n- SCADA puro.\\\\n- Tank/pump/pressure/flow interpretation excelente.\\\\n- Features documentadas.\\\\n- Target muy claro.\\\\n- La topología está disponible.\\\\n- Separación temporal excelente.\\\\n- Computacionalmente trivial.\\\\n- Ideal para Linear/KNN/Tree/RF/Boosting.\\\\n- Lags de 24 h tienen interpretación física.\\\\n- Muy poco trabajo Kaggle relativo.\\\\n- Muy fácil presentar el proyecto.\\\\n- Muy fácil hacer gráficos e interpretar resultados.\\\\n- Originalidad razonable si abandonamos attack classification.\\\\n- Train normal + attack dataset permite residual-based analysis.\\\\n\\\\n## Contras\\\\n\\\\n- Simulado.\\\\n- Sampling de 1 hora.\\\\n- Sólo ~15k observaciones totales.\\\\n- No representa bien transitorios rápidos de control.\\\\n- Apenas siete attack events en algunos subsets: muchos attack-labeled rows no equivalen a muchos ataques independientes. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"12\\\\\\\"}\\\\n- Si el proyecto se convierte en supervised attack classification, el tamaño efectivo de la clase minoritaria es pequeño.\\\\n- Al usar 24 lags × muchas variables, dimensionality puede crecer muy rápido.\\\\n\\\\n### Evaluación\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\\\\\textbf{91/100}}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nY hay algo importante:\\\\n\\\\n> **Para un mini-project que quieres terminar impecablemente, BATADAL puede ser incluso más seguro que SWaT.**\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# 🥉 3. WADI\\\\n\\\\n## Qué representa\\\\n\\\\nWADI es la pareja natural de SWaT.\\\\n\\\\nMientras SWaT trata **water treatment**, WADI representa **water distribution**.\\\\n\\\\nTiene tres stages:\\\\n\\\\n### P1 — Primary\\\\n\\\\nDos raw-water tanks de aproximadamente 2500 L.\\\\n\\\\n### P2 — Distribution\\\\n\\\\nDos elevated reservoirs que alimentan **seis consumer tanks**.\\\\n\\\\n### P3 — Return\\\\n\\\\nUnused water regresa y puede recircularse hacia P1. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"13\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEsto es tremendamente interesante desde control porque aparecen:\\\\n\\\\n- storage,\\\\n- consumer demand,\\\\n- pumps,\\\\n- reservoirs,\\\\n- return flow,\\\\n- pressure,\\\\n- level,\\\\n- valves.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Dataset\\\\n\\\\nLa referencia oficial registra:\\\\n\\\\n- **16 días continuos**\\\\n- 14 normales\\\\n- 2 con ataques\\\\n- **123 sensores y actuadores**\\\\n- 15 ataques. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"14\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nFuentes posteriores describen 69 sensores + 54 actuadores y muestreo de **1 segundo**. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"15\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEstamos nuevamente alrededor de un millón de observaciones.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Por qué técnicamente podría ser el #1\\\\n\\\\nWADI posiblemente tiene la dinámica más interesante de los cuatro.\\\\n\\\\nEn una red de distribución:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Demand}\\\\n+\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Pump operation}\\\\n+\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Storage}\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Levels/flows/pressures}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ny estas relaciones cambian dependiendo del operating regime.\\\\n\\\\nPodríamos hacer algo P_{consumer}(t+h)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nEso es prácticamente:\\\\n\\\\n### SCADA data-driven dynamic modeling.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Incluso más interesante\\\\n\\\\nComparar diferentes horizons:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nh=\\\\n10s,\\\\\\\\;30s,\\\\\\\\;60s,\\\\\\\\;5min\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ny observar:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nRMSE(h)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nA medida que aumenta \\\\\\\\(h\\\\\\\\), debería hacerse más difícil.\\\\n\\\\nEso es una pregunta científica clara.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Entonces, ¿por qué sólo #3?\\\\n\\\\nPor **riesgo de
